a_{1}^{\gamma_{\ell-1}+1}&a_{2}^{\gamma_{\ell-1}+1}&\cdots&a_{\ell-1}^{\gamma_{\ell-1}+1}&a_{\ell}^{\gamma_{\ell-1}+1}\\
a_{1}^{\gamma_{\ell}}&a_{2}^{\gamma_{\ell}}&\cdots&a_{\ell-1}^{\gamma_{\ell}}&a_{\ell}^{\gamma_{\ell}}\end{bmatrix}.

After deriving the explicit expansion, we need to employ the following two properties of the Schur polynomial
[
Mac15
]
to constrain the higher-order terms in
Section

D.2
:

Proposition D.2
(Properties of Schur polynomial).

Let
\boldsymbol{\gamma}=(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{\ell})\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
be a collection of non-increasingly ordered nonnegative integers.
The Schur polynomial
s_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{\ell})
satisfies the following properties:

1.

Polynomial.

s_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
is a symmetric, homogeneous polynomial of degree
\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}
. And the maximum degree of any variable
x_{i}
is
\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{\infty}
.

2.

Coefficients.
The coefficients of
s_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
are nonnegative integers and sum to

s_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(1,1,\ldots,1)=\prod_{i<j}\frac{\gamma_{i}-\gamma_{j}+j-i}{j-i}.

In particular, for any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

s_{(m,m,\ldots,m,0)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{\ell})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\{0,1,\dots,m\}^{\ell}:\\
\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{1}=(\ell-1)m\end{subarray}}~\prod_{i\in[\ell]}x_{i}^{\beta_{i}}\,,

and

s_{(m,m,\ldots,m,0)}(1,1,\ldots,1)=\prod_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\frac{m+\ell-i}{\ell-i}=\binom{m+\ell-1}{\ell-1}\quad\text{for }m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}.

We also need to use the standard cofactor expansion for the determinant calculation.

Fact D.3
(Cofactor expansion of determinant).

For any
n
-by-
n
matrix
A
, the cofactor expansion of the determinant along the first row is

\displaystyle\det(\boldsymbol{A})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{i-1}\det(\boldsymbol{A}_{\backslash i})\cdot\boldsymbol{A}_{1,i}\,,

where
\boldsymbol{A}_{\backslash i}
is a sub-matrix of
\boldsymbol{A}
by removing the first row and the
i
th column.

To streamline notation, we define:

\begin{aligned} &\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0}\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}};\quad&&\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{j}\coloneqq\boldsymbol{Q}(:,j)\boldsymbol{Q}(:,j)^{\dagger}\\
&\lambda_{0}\coloneqq 0;\quad&&\lambda_{j}\coloneqq\lambda_{j}(\boldsymbol{T})\end{aligned}\quad\quad\quad\text{for }j=1,\dots,r\,.

Now, we proceed to derive the polynomial
\textbf{Poly}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{r}}
. Initially, we naively decompose the matrix contour integrals outlined in
Proposition

4.2
into summations, where each term is a multiplication of eigenvector projections. In particular, we have

\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{r}}=\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}A_{i_{1},i_{2},\dots,i_{k+1}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\{0,1\ldots,r\}^{k+1}}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{1}}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{2}}\cdots\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{k+1}}\,.

(D.2)

We observe that
A_{i_{1},i_{2},\dots,i_{k+1}}
in each term can be expressed as scalar contour integrals, which can be explicitly written using Schur polynomials. The result is summarized as follows:

Proposition D.4
(Contour integral).

For any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,
m\geq-1
, and
\boldsymbol{i}\in\{1,\ldots,r\}^{\ell}
,

A_{m}(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})\coloneqq\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{r}}\frac{1}{(\zeta-\lambda_{i_{1}})\cdots(\zeta-\lambda_{i_{\ell}})\zeta^{m+1}}\,\mathrm{d}\zeta=(-1)^{\ell-1}\frac{s_{(m,m,\ldots,m,0)}(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})}{\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}\lambda_{i_{j}}^{m+1}},

(D.3)

where
\mathcal{C}_{r}
denotes the contour in
Proposition

4.2
.

Proof.

The function
A_{m}
is continuous on the interior of
\mathcal{C}_{r}
.
Consequently, we are free to assume without loss of generality that
\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}}
are distinct.
The formula we derive will then extend to all
\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}}
in the interior of
\mathcal{C}_{r}
by continuity.

Using Cauchy’s residue theorem (see e.g.
[
SS10
]
) to evaluate the contour integral in
Eq.

D.3
, we obtain

A_{m}(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\frac{1}{\lambda_{i_{j}}^{m+1}}\prod_{p\neq j}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{i_{j}}-\lambda_{i_{p}})}

To consolidate this sum, we multiply by the numerator and denominator of the
j
-th summand by
(-1)^{j-1}W(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{j-1}},\lambda_{i_{j+1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})\prod_{p\neq j}\lambda_{i_{p}}^{m+1}
, resulting in

\displaystyle A_{m}(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})=

\displaystyle~\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}W(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{j-1}},\lambda_{i_{j+1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})\prod_{p\neq j}\lambda_{i_{p}}^{m+1}}{(-1)^{j-1}W(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{j-1}},\lambda_{i_{j+1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})\prod_{p\neq j}\lambda_{i_{p}}^{m+1}\cdot\lambda_{i_{j}}^{m+1}\prod_{p\neq j}(\lambda_{i_{j}}-\lambda_{i_{p}})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}(-1)^{j-1}W(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{j-1}},\lambda_{i_{j+1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})\prod_{p\neq j}\lambda_{i_{p}}^{m+1}}{W(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})\prod_{p=1}^{\ell}\lambda_{i_{p}}^{m+1}}\,,

where the second step follows from

\displaystyle W(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{j-1}},\lambda_{i_{j+1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})\cdot\prod_{p\neq j}(\lambda_{i_{j}}-\lambda_{i_{p}})=

\displaystyle~\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha<\beta\\
\alpha,\beta\neq j\end{subarray}}(\lambda_{i_{\alpha}}-\lambda_{i_{\beta}})\cdot\prod_{p\neq j}(\lambda_{i_{j}}-\lambda_{i_{p}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(-1)^{j-1}\prod_{\alpha<\beta}(\lambda_{i_{\alpha}}-\lambda_{i_{\beta}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(-1)^{j-1}W(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})\,.

We recognize the numerator of this expression of a cofactor expansion of a determinant (
Fact

D.3
), obtaining

A_{m}(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})=\frac{D(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})}{W(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})\prod_{p=1}^{\ell}\lambda_{i_{p}}^{m+1}}

(D.4)

where

D(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})\coloneqq\det\begin{bmatrix}\prod_{p\neq 1}\lambda_{i_{p}}^{m+1}&\prod_{p\neq 2}\lambda_{i_{p}}^{m+1}&\cdots&\prod_{p\neq\ell-1}\lambda_{i_{p}}^{m+1}&\prod_{p\neq\ell}\lambda_{i_{p}}^{m+1}\\
\lambda_{i_{1}}^{\ell-2}&\lambda_{i_{2}}^{\ell-2}&\cdots&\lambda_{i_{\ell-1}}^{\ell-2}&\lambda_{i_{\ell}}^{\ell-2}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\lambda_{i_{1}}^{1}&\lambda_{i_{2}}^{1}&\cdots&\lambda_{i_{\ell-1}}^{1}&\lambda_{i_{\ell}}^{1}\\
1&1&\cdots&1&1\end{bmatrix}\,.

By pulling out a factor of
\prod_{p=1}^{\ell}\lambda_{i_{p}}^{m+1}
from the first row, we obtain

D(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})=\prod_{p=1}^{\ell}\lambda_{i_{p}}^{m+1}\cdot\det\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{i_{1}}^{-(m+1)}&\lambda_{i_{2}}^{-(m+1)}&\cdots&\lambda_{i_{\ell-1}}^{-(m+1)}&\lambda_{i_{\ell}}^{-(m+1)}\\
\lambda_{i_{1}}^{\ell-2}&\lambda_{i_{2}}^{\ell-2}&\cdots&\lambda_{i_{\ell-1}}^{\ell-2}&\lambda_{i_{\ell}}^{\ell-2}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\lambda_{i_{1}}^{1}&\lambda_{i_{2}}^{1}&\cdots&\lambda_{i_{\ell-1}}^{1}&\lambda_{i_{\ell}}^{1}\\
1&1&\cdots&1&1\end{bmatrix}.

Now shuffle the first row to the bottom of the matrix and absorb a factor of
\lambda_{i_{j}}^{m+1}
into each column
j
, obtaining

\displaystyle D(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell-1}\det\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{i_{1}}^{m+\ell-1}&\lambda_{i_{2}}^{m+\ell-1}&\cdots&\lambda_{i_{\ell-1}}^{m+\ell-1}&\lambda_{i_{\ell}}^{m+\ell-1}\\
\lambda_{i_{1}}^{m+\ell-2}&\lambda_{i_{2}}^{m+\ell-2}&\cdots&\lambda_{i_{\ell-1}}^{m+\ell-2}&\lambda_{i_{\ell}}^{m+\ell-2}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\lambda_{i_{1}}^{m+1}&\lambda_{i_{2}}^{m+1}&\cdots&\lambda_{i_{\ell-1}}^{m+1}&\lambda_{i_{\ell}}^{m+1}\\
1&1&\cdots&1&1\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell-1}s_{(m,m,\ldots,m,0)}(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})W(\lambda_{i_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{\ell}})\,,

where the second step follows from the definition of the Schur polynomial (
Definition

D.1
).
Substituting this expression back in
Eq.

D.4
yields the stated result.
∎

An important observation stemming from
Proposition

D.4
is that
A_{m}
is a polynomial of
\lambda_{i}
for
i\neq 0
. Thus, when inserting
A_{m}
back into Eq. (
D.2
), we can see each
\lambda_{i}
can be a variable
x
that varies within the set
\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j=1}^{r}
rather than as a fixed eigenvalue. Based on this intuition, we introduce the following definition.

Definition D.5
(Interleaving evaluation).

For a function
f
which is a polynomial in the inverses
x_{1}^{-1},\ldots,x_{k+1}^{-1}
of
k+1
variables, define the
\boldsymbol{T},\boldsymbol{E},\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0}
-
interleaving evaluation
for any monomial
x_{1}^{-\beta_{1}}x_{2}^{-\beta_{2}}\cdots x_{k+1}^{-\beta_{k+1}}
as:

\displaystyle\left(x_{1}^{-\beta_{1}}x_{2}^{-\beta_{2}}\cdots x_{k+1}^{-\beta_{k+1}}\right)[\boldsymbol{T},\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0},\boldsymbol{E}]\coloneqq

\displaystyle\quad\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0}\right)^{[\beta_{1}=0]}\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{\beta_{1}}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{E}\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0}\right)^{[\beta_{2}=0]}\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{\beta_{2}}\right)\cdots\boldsymbol{E}\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0}\right)^{[\beta_{k+1}=0]}\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{\beta_{k+1}}\right)\,,

where
[\beta_{i}=0]=1
if
\beta_{i}=0
and
[\beta_{i}=0]=0
otherwise.
And the
\boldsymbol{T},\boldsymbol{E},\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0}
-
interleaving evaluation
for the general polynomial
f
is defined by linearity.

Remark D.6
(Example of interleaving evaluation).

For expository purposes, we give one example for above definition:

(x_{1}^{-1}x_{2}^{0}x_{3}^{-1}+x_{1}^{0}x_{2}^{0}x_{3}^{-2})[\boldsymbol{T},\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0},\boldsymbol{E}]=\boldsymbol{T}^{+}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{T}^{+}+\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0}\boldsymbol{E}\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{2}.

We see that each variable
x_{i}^{-\beta}
raised to a negative power leads to a power of the pseudoinverse
\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{\beta}
in the expression.
Similarly, each variable
x_{i}^{0}
raised to the zero power yields a copy of
\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0}
.
Finally, these powers of
\boldsymbol{T}^{+}
and
\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0p}
are interleaved by copies of the matrix
\boldsymbol{E}
.

Finally, using the definition of the interleaving evaluation,
Proposition

4.2
and Eq. (
D.2
) which expand
\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{\widehat{Q}}_{r}}
in terms of contour integrals, and
Proposition

D.4
to evaluating those contour integrals, we obtain an explicit form for
\textbf{Poly}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{r}}
in Eq. (
D.1
):

Theorem D.7
(Expansion of spectral projector, explicit form).

It holds that

\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{\widehat{Q}}_{r}}=\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\mathcal{J}\subseteq\{1,\ldots,k+1\}}\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}

where

\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}\coloneqq\left(A_{k-|\mathcal{J}|}(x_{i}:i\in\mathcal{J})\right)[\boldsymbol{T},\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0},\boldsymbol{E}]\,,

(D.5)

where
A_{m}
is the function defined in
Eq.

D.3
.

The above theorem can be intuitively derived by exploiting the polynomial structure of the coefficients in Equation Eq. (
D.2
). For example, consider any term
A_{i_{1},i_{2},0\dots,0}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{1}}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{2}}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0}\cdots\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0}
with
i_{1}\neq 0
and
i_{2}\neq 0
. Since
A_{i_{1},i_{2},0\dots,0}
is a polynomial of
\lambda^{-1}_{i_{1}}
and
\lambda^{-1}_{i_{2}}
, as demonstrated in
Proposition

D.4
, adding up
1\leq i_{1},i_{2}\leq r
yields
\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\{1,2\}}
.

Proof.

We begin by defining some notation.
Fix an index
k\geq 1
and let

\boldsymbol{C}_{k}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{r}}(\zeta-\boldsymbol{T})^{-1}\left(\boldsymbol{E}(\zeta-\boldsymbol{T})^{-1}\right)^{k}\,\mathrm{d}\zeta\,.

Our goal in this proof will be to show that
\boldsymbol{C}_{k}=\sum_{\mathcal{J}\subseteq\{1,\ldots,k+1\}}\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}
.
By the spectral theorem, the resolvent
(\zeta-\boldsymbol{T})^{-1}
has the spectral decomposition

(\zeta-\boldsymbol{T})^{-1}=\sum_{j=0}^{r}(\zeta-\lambda_{j})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{j}.

(D.6)

Using the spectral expansion
Eq.

D.6
in the definition of
\boldsymbol{C}_{k}
, we obtain

\boldsymbol{C}_{k}=\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\{0,1\ldots,r\}^{k+1}}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{1}}\cdot\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{2}}\cdots\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{k+1}}\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{r}}\frac{1}{(\zeta-\lambda_{i_{1}})\cdots(\zeta-\lambda_{i_{k+1}})}\,\mathrm{d}\zeta.

To evaluate this sum, re-index the sum by the support
\mathcal{J}
of the index vector
\boldsymbol{i}
, obtaining

\boldsymbol{C}_{k}=\sum_{\mathcal{J}\subseteq\{1,\ldots,k+1\}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{j}=0\text{ for }j\notin\mathcal{J}\\
i_{j}\in\{1,\ldots,r\}\text{ for }j\in\mathcal{J}\end{subarray}}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{1}}\cdot\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{2}}\cdots\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{k+1}}\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{r}}\frac{1}{\zeta^{(k-|\mathcal{J}|)+1}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}(\zeta-\lambda_{i_{j}})}\,\mathrm{d}\zeta.

By
Proposition

D.4
, the contour integral in this expression can be evaluated as:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{r}}\frac{1}{\zeta^{(k-|\mathcal{J}|)+1}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}(\zeta-\lambda_{i_{j}})}\,\mathrm{d}\zeta=A_{k-|\mathcal{J}|}(\lambda_{i_{j}}:j\in\mathcal{J})\,,

where
A_{k-|\mathcal{J}|}
is defined by
Eq.

D.3
. We may recognize
A_{k-|\mathcal{J}|}(x_{j}:j\in{\cal J})
as a
(k+1)
-variate polynomial of
x_{1}^{-1},\dots,x_{k+1}^{-1}
(by multiplying
\prod_{j\notin{\cal J}}x_{j}^{0}
). Then, we have

\displaystyle A_{k-|\mathcal{J}|}(\lambda_{i_{j}}:j\in\mathcal{J})=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathbb{N}^{k+1}}A_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{k+1}\lambda_{i_{j}}^{-\beta_{j}}\,,

where
A_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathbb{R}
is the coefficient.
Notice that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{j}=0\text{ for }j\notin\mathcal{J}\\
i_{j}\in\{1,\ldots,r\}\text{ for }j\in\mathcal{J}\end{subarray}}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{1}}\cdot\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{2}}\cdots\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{k+1}}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{k+1}\lambda_{i_{j}}^{-\beta_{j}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left(\sum_{i_{1}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{1}}}{\lambda_{i_{1}}^{\beta_{1}}}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{E}\left(\sum_{i_{2}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{2}}}{\lambda_{i_{2}}^{\beta_{2}}}\right)\cdots\boldsymbol{E}\left(\sum_{i_{k+1}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{k+1}}}{\lambda_{i_{k+1}}^{\beta_{k+1}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0}\right)^{[\beta_{1}=0]}\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{\beta_{1}}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{E}\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0}\right)^{[\beta_{2}=0]}\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{\beta_{2}}\right)\cdots\boldsymbol{E}\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0}\right)^{[\beta_{k+1}=0]}\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{\beta_{k+1}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k+1}x_{j}^{-\beta_{j}}\right)[\boldsymbol{T},\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0},\boldsymbol{E}]\,,

where the summation of
i_{j}
in the second step depends on whether
j\in{\cal J}
, the third step follows from
\sum_{j=1}^{r}\lambda_{j}^{-t}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{j}=(\boldsymbol{T}^{+})^{t}
for any
t>0
, and the last step follows from
Definition

D.5
.
By linearity, it implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{j}=0\text{ for }j\notin\mathcal{J}\\
i_{j}\in\{1,\ldots,r\}\text{ for }j\in\mathcal{J}\end{subarray}}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{1}}\cdot\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{2}}\cdots\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i_{k+1}}\cdot A_{k-|\mathcal{J}|}(\lambda_{i_{j}}:j\in\mathcal{J})=\left(A_{k-|\mathcal{J}|}(x_{i}:i\in\mathcal{J})\right)[\boldsymbol{T},\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0},\boldsymbol{E}]\,.

Thus, we obtain that

\boldsymbol{C}_{k}=\sum_{\mathcal{J}\subseteq\{1,\ldots,k+1\}}\left(A_{k-|\mathcal{J}|}(x_{i}:i\in\mathcal{J})\right)[\boldsymbol{T},\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{0},\boldsymbol{E}]=\sum_{\mathcal{J}\subseteq\{1,\ldots,k+1\}}\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}\,.

The theorem then follows by
Proposition

4.2
.
∎

D.2
Expansion of spectral projector: bounds on terms

In this section, we bound the terms appearing in the expansion of the spectral projector,
Theorem

D.7
.
Our result is as follows:

Lemma D.8
(Terms in expansion of spectral projector).

Fix a number
k\geq 2
and a subset
\mathcal{J}\subseteq\{1,\ldots,k+1\}
.
Then there exists a constant
0<C<3/8
such that

(a)

If
\mathcal{J}=\emptyset
, then
\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}=\boldsymbol{0}
.

(b)

If
|\mathcal{J}|\geq 2
, then

\left\|\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}+\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{n}}\right)^{|\mathcal{J}|}C^{k-|\mathcal{J}|}.

(c)

If
\mathcal{J}=\{j\}
for
2\leq i\leq k
, then

\left\|\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}n^{2}}+\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu_{r}^{2}n}\right)C^{k-2}.

(d)

For
\mathcal{J}=\{1\}
and
\mathcal{J}=\{k+1\}
, we have the representations

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\{1\}}=\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm random}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}(\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}})^{k-1}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)C^{k-1}\left\|\boldsymbol{G}_{1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\{k+1\}}=(\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}\boldsymbol{E})^{k-1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm random}\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{k}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)C^{k-1}\left\|\boldsymbol{G}_{2}\right\|_{2}

where
\left\|\boldsymbol{G}_{1}\right\|_{2},\left\|\boldsymbol{G}_{2}\right\|_{2}=1
.
For the leading-order terms, we have the bounds

\begin{split}\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm random}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}(\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}})^{k-1}\right\|_{2}&=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}\mu_{r}}\right)C^{k-1}\\
\left\|(\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}\boldsymbol{E})^{k-1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm random}\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{k}\right\|_{2}&=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}\mu_{r}}\right)C^{k-1}.\end{split}

(D.7)

Proof.

For ease of reading, we break the proof into steps.

Proof of (a).

The conclusion
\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\emptyset}=\boldsymbol{0}
follows directly from the formula
Eq.

D.5
and
A_{k-\emptyset}=0
.

Expression of
\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}
as a sum of monomials.

For the remaining parts of the result, we shall decompose
\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}
as a sum of monomials.
By
Proposition

D.2
and the definition of
\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}
,
\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}
is equal to
(-1)^{|\mathcal{J}|-1}
times a polynomial with degree
-k
. More specifically,

\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}=(-1)^{|\mathcal{J}|-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathbb{N}^{k+1},\sum^{k+1}_{i=1}\beta_{i}=k\\
\left\{j|\beta_{j}\neq 0\right\}\subseteq\mathcal{J}\end{subarray}}A_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\cdot\left(x_{1}^{-\beta_{1}}\cdots x_{k+1}^{-\beta_{k+1}}\right)[\boldsymbol{T},\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}},\boldsymbol{E}],

(D.8)

where the coefficients
A_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
are nonnegative integers. In addition, according to the second point of
Proposition

D.2
, we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathbb{N}^{k+1},\sum^{k+1}_{i=1}\beta_{i}=k\\
\left\{j|\beta_{j}\neq 0\right\}\subseteq\mathcal{J}\end{subarray}}A_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=\binom{k-1}{|\mathcal{J}|-1}

(D.9)

In view of the identities
\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{\alpha}=\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{\alpha}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}
and
\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}=\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}\cdot\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}
, we can write

\left(x_{1}^{-\beta_{1}}\cdots x_{k+1}^{-\beta_{k+1}}\right)[\boldsymbol{T},\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}},\boldsymbol{E}]=\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{\beta_{1}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\left[(\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j+1})\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{\beta_{j+1}}\right]

where
\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j}=\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}
if
j\in\mathcal{J}
and
\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j}=\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}
otherwise.
Here, we use the convention
\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{0}=\mathbf{I}_{n}
.

Bounding each monomial.

By the submultiplicative property of
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{2}
, we have a norm bound on each of these monomials.

\begin{split}\left\|\left(x_{1}^{-\beta_{1}}\cdots x_{k+1}^{-\beta_{k+1}}\right)[\boldsymbol{T},\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}},\boldsymbol{E}]\right\|_{2}\leq&~\left\|\boldsymbol{T}^{+}\right\|^{k}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j+1}\right\|_{2}\\
=&~\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{r}n}\right)^{k}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j+1}\right\|_{2}\,,\end{split}

(D.10)

where the second step follows from
\left\|\boldsymbol{T}^{+}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{\mu_{r}n})
by
Corollary

B.3
.

To bound the factors
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j+1}\right\|_{2}
, we consider two cases. When
j\in{\cal J}
or
j+1\in{\cal J}
, this term is either
\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\|_{2}
or
\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\|_{2}
, which can be upper-bounded by
\|\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\|_{2}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\|_{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm tail}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\|_{2}+\|\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm random}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\|_{2}\leq{\cal O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}}\right)+\|\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm tail}\|_{2}={\cal O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}}+\alpha\sqrt{n\log n}\right)\,,

where the second step follows from
Proposition

C.3
and the third step follows from
Lemma

C.1
. Otherwise, this term becomes
\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}}\|_{2}
. By
Proposition

C.2
,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}\|_{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{E}\|_{2}=n\mu_{\rm tail}+{\cal O}\left(\alpha\sqrt{n\log n}\right)\,.

Therefore, we obtain that

\left\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j+1}\right\|_{2}\leq\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}}+\alpha\sqrt{n\log n}\right)&\text{if }j\in\mathcal{J}\text{ or }j+1\in\mathcal{J},\\
n\mu_{\rm tail}+\mathcal{O}\left(\alpha\sqrt{n\log n}\right)&\text{for any value of $j$}.\end{cases}.

(D.11)

Given any
\mathcal{J}
, we notice that at least
|\mathcal{J}|
of the terms
\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j+1}
satisfy
j\in\mathcal{J}
or
j+1\in\mathcal{J}
.
Thus, substituting the bounds
Eq.

D.11
into
Eq.

D.10
, we obtain

\left\|\left(x_{1}^{-\beta_{1}}\cdots x_{k+1}^{-\beta_{k+1}}\right)[\boldsymbol{T},\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}},\boldsymbol{E}]\right\|_{2}\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}+\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{n}}\right)^{|\mathcal{J}|}a^{k-|\mathcal{J}|},

(D.12)

where

0<a\leq\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\mu_{r}}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{n}}\right)<\frac{3}{16}

is an absolute constant.
Define
C\coloneqq 2a
. Now, we are ready to show (b)–(d):

Proof of (b).

Using Eq. (
D.12
) and Eq. (
D.9
) to bound every term in Eq. (
D.8
), we conclude that

\left\|\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}\right\|_{2}\leq\binom{k-1}{|\mathcal{J}|-1}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}+\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{n}}\right)^{|\mathcal{J}|}a^{k-|\mathcal{J}|}\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}+\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{n}}\right)^{|\mathcal{J}|}C^{k-|\mathcal{J}|}\,,

where we use the trivial bound
\binom{m}{p}\leq 2^{m}
in the second inequality. This proves (b).

Proof of (c).

If
\mathcal{J}=\{j\}
for some
2\leq j\leq k
, we know that
A_{k-1}(x_{j})=x_{j}^{-k}
. Then,
Eq.

D.11
implies

\left\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j-1}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j}\right\|_{2},~~\left\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j+1}\right\|_{2}\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}}+\alpha\sqrt{n\log n}\right).

Similar to Eq. (
D.12
), using the second bound in
Eq.

D.11
for the other
k-2
factors in
Eq.

D.10
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\left(x_{1}^{0}\cdots x_{j-1}^{0}x_{j}^{-k}x_{j+1}^{0}\cdots x_{k+1}^{0}\right)[\boldsymbol{T},\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}},\boldsymbol{E}]\right\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle~\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}+\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{n}}\right)^{2}a^{k-2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}+\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{n}}\right)^{2}C^{k-2}

Since
\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}
is a single monomial of the form we just bounded, so part (c) is proven.

Proof of (d).

If
\mathcal{J}=\{1\}
or
\mathcal{J}=\{k+1\}
, then the projector
\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j}
is on the end, so only the first or the last factor
\|\boldsymbol{T}^{+}\|\left\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}({\cal J})_{j+1}\right\|_{2}
is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(1/\sqrt{n})
and the other factors are
\mathcal{O}(1)
. Thus, this term gives us
\mathcal{O}(1/\sqrt{n})
. We need to obtain its specific formula.

We treat the case
\mathcal{J}=\{1\}
, with the case
\mathcal{J}=\{k+1\}
following similarly.
Using our formula for
\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\{1\}}
and the decomposition
\boldsymbol{E}=\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm random}+\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm tail}
, we have that

\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\{1\}}=\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}(\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}})^{k-1}=\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm random}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}(\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}})^{k-1}+\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm tail}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}(\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}})^{k-1}\,.

For the first term, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm random}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}(\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}})^{k-1}\right\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle~\left\|\boldsymbol{T}^{+}\right\|^{k}_{2}\cdot\left\|\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm random}\right\|_{2}\cdot\left\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}(\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}})^{k-1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~{\cal O}\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{r}n}\right)^{k}\cdot{\cal O}\left(\alpha\sqrt{n\log n}\right)\cdot\left(n\mu_{\rm tail}+{\cal O}\left(\alpha\sqrt{n\log n}\right)\right)^{k-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}\mu_{r}}\right)C^{k-1},

where the second step follows from
Corollaries

B.3
,
C.1
and
D.11
.

For the second term, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm tail}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}(\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}})^{k-1}\right\|_{2}=

\displaystyle~\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm tail}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}(\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}})^{k-1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{k}\right\|_{2}\cdot\left\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm tail}\right\|_{2}\cdot\left\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}(\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}})^{k-1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~{\cal O}\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{r}n}\right)^{k}\cdot{\cal O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}}\right)\cdot\left(n\mu_{\rm tail}+{\cal O}\left(\alpha\sqrt{n\log n}\right)\right)^{k-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)C^{k-1}\,,

where we use Eq. (
C.3
) for
\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm tail}
in the third step.
This concludes the proof of (d).
∎

D.3
Proof of
Lemma

4.3

See
4.3

Proof.

Begin with the explicit expansion of the spectral projector (
Theorem

D.7
):

\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{\widehat{Q}}_{r}}=\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\mathcal{J}\subseteq\{1,\ldots,k+1\}}\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}.

The summands
\boldsymbol{F}_{k,\mathcal{J}}
are defined in
Eq.

D.5
.
Now use
Lemma

D.8
to obtain

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{\widehat{Q}}_{r}}=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left(\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm random}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}(\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}})^{k-1}+(\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}\boldsymbol{E})^{k-1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm random}\left({\boldsymbol{T}}^{+}\right)^{k}\right)

(D.13)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Bigg[\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)C^{k-1}+k\cdot\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}n^{2}}+\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu_{r}^{2}n}\right)C^{k-2}+D_{k}\Bigg]\boldsymbol{G}\,.

with
\left\|\boldsymbol{G}\right\|_{2}\leq 1
and

D_{k}\coloneqq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{J}\subseteq\{1,\ldots,k+1\}\\
|\mathcal{J}|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}+\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{n}}\right)^{|\mathcal{J}|}C^{k-|\mathcal{J}|}.

To bound
D_{k}
, begin by rewriting this as a sum over the size
t
of the subset
\mathcal{J}
, of which there are
\binom{k+1}{t}
such subsets.
Therefore,

D_{k}=\sum_{t=2}^{k+1}\binom{k+1}{t}\cdot\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}+\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{n}}\right)^{t}C^{k-t}\,.

Now invoke the trivial bound
\binom{m}{p}\leq 2^{m}
to obtain

D_{k}=\sum_{t=2}^{k+1}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}+\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{n}}\right)^{t}(2C)^{k-t}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}n^{2}}+\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu_{r}^{2}n}\right)\cdot(2C)^{k}\,.

Substituting in
Eq.

D.13
and summing over
k
, the last term becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Bigg[\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)C^{k-1}+k\cdot\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}n^{2}}+\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu_{r}^{2}n}\right)C^{k-2}+D_{k}\Bigg]\boldsymbol{G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Bigg[\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)C^{k-1}+k\cdot\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}n^{2}}+\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu_{r}^{2}n}\right)C^{k-2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}n^{2}}+\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu_{r}^{2}n}\right)\cdot(2C)^{k}\Bigg]\boldsymbol{G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{\cal O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}+\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu_{r}^{2}n}\right)\boldsymbol{G}\,,

where the last step follows from
C\in(0,3/8)
.

The lemma is then proved.
∎

Appendix E
Deferred proofs for
Section

5

E.1
Proof for Step 1: Construction of the “good”
\boldsymbol{P}

See
5.2

Proof.

By the eigenvector perturbation lemma (
Lemma

4.1
), we obtain that there exists a unitary matrix
\boldsymbol{U}_{r}\in\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}
such that

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{r}\boldsymbol{U}_{r}=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}+\underbrace{\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}_{r}\right)^{k+1}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}}\right)\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{1}}_{\text{first order term}}+\underbrace{\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu_{r}^{2}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}}_{\text{second order term}}

(E.1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\left[\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{z}n}}\right)\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{1}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu^{2}_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}\right]}_{\text{in the column space of $\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}$}}

\displaystyle+\left[\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu^{2}_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}\right]

where
\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}
is the projection onto the column space of
\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}
and
\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}
is the projection onto the column space that is orthogonal to the column space of
\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}
, and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{1},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times r}
satisfying
\left\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{1}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
and
\left\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
.

By
Claim

E.1
, the first-order error term that is orthogonal to the column space of
\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}
can be simplified as follows:

\displaystyle\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}=

\displaystyle~\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\left(\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}\right)\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle~{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n^{1.5}}\right)\,.

(E.2)

It gives that

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{r}\boldsymbol{U}_{r}=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{z}n}}\right)\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{1}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu^{2}_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}

(E.3)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\left(\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}\right)\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\cal{\displaystyle O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu^{2}_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n^{1.5}}\right)\,.

Because the image of
\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{z}n}}\right)\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{1}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu^{2}_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}
equals to the image of
\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}
, there exists an invertible matrix
\boldsymbol{P}\in\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}
such that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{z}n}}\right)\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{1}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu^{2}_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}=\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\boldsymbol{P}\,.

Multiplying
\boldsymbol{Q}^{\dagger}_{r}
on both sides, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{P}:=

\displaystyle~\boldsymbol{Q}^{\dagger}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{z}n}}\right)\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{1}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu^{2}_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\boldsymbol{I}_{r}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{z}n}}\right)\boldsymbol{Q}^{\dagger}_{r}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{1}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu^{2}_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\boldsymbol{Q}^{\dagger}_{r}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}\,,

which gives the expression of
\boldsymbol{P}
Eq. (
5.2
) in the lemma.

Property I and II follow from
Claim

E.2
.
The proof of the lemma is then completed.
∎

E.1.1
Technical claims

Claim E.1
(Simplifying the first-order error term).

The first-order error term in the orthogonal space of
\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}
in Eq. (
E.1
) can be expressed as:

\displaystyle\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}=

\displaystyle~\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\left(\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}\right)\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}

\displaystyle~+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n^{1.5}}\right)\,.

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\dagger}

(E.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{P}_{v}\boldsymbol{P}_{v}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}+\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\left(\boldsymbol{P}_{v}\boldsymbol{P}_{v}^{\dagger}-I_{r}\right)\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}+\left\|\boldsymbol{P}_{v}\boldsymbol{P}_{v}^{\dagger}-I_{r}\right\|_{2}\cdot\left\|\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\cdot\left\|\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\,,

where the first step follows from
\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\dagger}=\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}
, the second step follows from Eq. (
1.13
), the third and the fourth steps are straightforward, the fifth step follows from
Lemma

1.8
, and the last step follows from
Corollary

B.2
that
\left\|\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}={\cal O}\left(1+\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)={\cal O}(1)
.
Then, by triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}-\left(\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}\right)\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}-\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle~+\left\|\left(\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}-\Big(\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}\Big)\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}\right\|_{2}\,.

For the first term, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}-\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\|\left(\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\right)^{k}-\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\|\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\right)^{k}-\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\right\|_{2}\cdot{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{n\log n}}{(n\mu_{r})^{k+1}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left\|\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\right)^{k}-\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}+\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\right)\right)^{k}\right\|_{2}\cdot{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{n\log n}}{(n\mu_{r})^{k+1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sum_{l=1}^{k}\binom{k}{l}\cdot\left\|\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\right\|_{2}^{l}\cdot\left\|\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\right\|_{2}^{k-l}\cdot{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{n\log n}}{(n\mu_{r})^{k+1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sum_{l=1}^{k}\binom{k}{l}\cdot{\cal O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}}\right)^{l}\cdot{\cal O}(n\mu_{\rm tail})^{k-l}\cdot{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{n\log n}}{(n\mu_{r})^{k+1}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\binom{k-1}{l}\cdot{\cal O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}}\right)^{l}\cdot{\cal O}(n\mu_{\rm tail})^{k-1-l}\cdot k\cdot\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}\alpha\sqrt{n\log n}}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}(n\mu_{r})^{k+1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{\cal O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}}+n\mu_{\rm tail}\right)^{k-1}\cdot k\cdot\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}\alpha\sqrt{n\log n}}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}(n\mu_{r})^{k+1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{\cal O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\mu_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}+\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\mu_{r}}\right)^{k-1}k\cdot\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}^{2}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n^{1.5}}\,,

where the first step follows from
\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}\|_{2}=1
, the second step follows from
\|\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\|_{2}={\cal O}(\alpha\sqrt{n\log n})
and
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\|_{2}=(n\mu_{r})^{-1}
by Eq. (
B.2
) in
Corollary

B.3
, the third step follows from
\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}+\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}=\boldsymbol{I}_{r}
, the fourth step follows from the binomial expansion and triangle inequality, the fifth step follows from
\|\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}\|_{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\|_{2}={\cal O}(n\mu_{\rm tail})
by Eq. (
C.1
)
and
\|\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\|_{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\|_{2}={\cal O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}}\right)
by
Proposition

C.3
,
the sixth step follows from changing the summation index, and the last two steps are direct calculations.

For the second term, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}-\Big(\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}\Big)\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{\cal O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\cdot\left\|\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{\cal O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\cdot{\cal O}(n\mu_{\rm tail})^{k}\cdot{\cal O}(\alpha\sqrt{n\log n})\cdot(n\mu_{r})^{-k-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{\cal O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\mu_{r}}\right)^{k}\cdot\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n^{1.5}}\,,

where the first step follows from Eq. (
E.4
), the second step follows from
\left\|\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(n\mu_{\rm tail}\right)
,
\left\|\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\alpha\sqrt{n\log(n)}\right)
, and
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\|_{2}=1/(n\mu_{r})
by Eq. (
B.2
) in
Corollary

B.3
.

Combining them together, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}-\left(\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}\right)\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~{\cal O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\mu_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}+\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\mu_{r}}\right)^{k-1}k\cdot\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}^{2}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n^{1.5}}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\mu_{r}}\right)^{k}\cdot\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n^{1.5}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{\cal O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\mu_{r}}\right)^{k}k\cdot\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n^{1.5}}\,,

where the second step follows from
n\geq 1/(\mu_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}})
.
Thus, summing over
k
gives that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}^{\perp}_{r}}\right)^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}-\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\left(\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}\right)\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}{\cal O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\mu_{r}}\right)^{k}k\cdot\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n^{1.5}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n^{1.5}}\right)\,.

where the last step follows from
{\cal O}(\mu_{\rm tail}/\mu_{r})<1
.

The claim is then proved.
∎

Claim E.2
(Properties of the “good”
\boldsymbol{P}
).

The invertible matrix
\boldsymbol{P}
defined by Eq. (
5.2
) satisfies the following properties:

•

Property I:

\left\|\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\right\|_{2}={\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{z}n}}\right)\,,\quad\left\|\boldsymbol{P}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\right\|_{2}={\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{z}n}}\right)\,.

(E.5)

•

Property II:

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{Q}}_{r}\boldsymbol{U}_{r}\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\right\|_{2}\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}}\right)\,.

(E.6)

Proof.

We prove each of the properties below.

Proof of Property I:

By the definition of
\boldsymbol{P}
(Eq. (
5.2
)), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{P}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\right\|_{2}=

\displaystyle~{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{z}n}}\right)\cdot\left\|\boldsymbol{Q}^{\dagger}_{r}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{1}\right\|_{2}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu_{r}^{2}\Delta_{z}n}\right)\cdot\left\|\boldsymbol{Q}^{\dagger}_{r}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}\right\|_{2}={\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{z}n}}\right)\,,

where the second step follows from
\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\|_{2}=1
,
\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{1}\|_{2}={\cal O}(1)
, and
\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}\|_{2}={\cal O}(1)
by their definitions. It also implies that

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\right\|_{2}={\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{z}n}}\right)\,.

Proof of Property II:

Consider

\widehat{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{r}\boldsymbol{U}_{r}\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}=\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{E}}^{Q}_{1}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{E}}^{Q}_{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n^{1.5}}\right)\,,

where

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{E}}^{Q}_{1}:=\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\left(\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}\right)\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}\,.

(E.7a)

and

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{E}}^{Q}_{2}:={\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu^{2}_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}\,.

(E.7b)

We first bound the norm of
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{E}}^{Q}_{1}
:

\displaystyle\big\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{E}}^{Q}_{1}\big\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}\cdot\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\left\|\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}\right\|_{2}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}\|_{2}

(E.8)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1)\cdot\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\left\|\boldsymbol{E}_{1}^{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\right)^{k+1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1)\cdot\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}{\cal O}\left(\frac{\mu_{\rm tail}}{\mu_{r}}\right)^{k}\cdot\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cal{\displaystyle O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{n}}\right)\,,

where the first step follows from triangle inequality, the second step follows from
Lemma

1.8
and
Property I
, the third step follows from
\left\|\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(n\mu_{\rm tail}\right)
,
\left\|\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\alpha\sqrt{n\log(n)}\right)
, and
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}^{-1}\|_{2}=1/(n\mu_{r})
by Eq. (
B.2
) in
Corollary

B.3
.

Then, we bound the norm of
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{E}}^{Q}_{2}
:

\displaystyle\big\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{E}}^{Q}_{2}\big\|_{2}\leq{\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu^{2}_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\cdot\|\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}\|_{2}={\cal O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu^{2}_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\,,

where the first step follows from
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}^{\perp}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
since
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{2}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
,
and the second step follows from
\|\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}\|_{2}={\cal O}(1)
by
Property I
.

Hence, we get that

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{Q}}_{r}\boldsymbol{U}_{r}\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{Q}_{r}\right\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\cal{\displaystyle O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu^{2}_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}+\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n^{1.5}}\right)

(E.9)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}}\right)\,.

∎

E.2
Proofs for Step 2: Taylor expansion with respect to the error terms

See
5.3

Proof.

According to Eq. (
5.4
) and the assumption that
n
is large enough, we have
\|\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}\boldsymbol{Q}\|_{2}<1
and
\|\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}\|_{2}<1
. Then, we can use the formula of pseudoinverse
Eq.

A.1
and Neumann series expansion (
Definition

A.6
) to derive

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{\widehat{Q}}^{+}_{\downarrow}\boldsymbol{\widehat{Q}}_{\uparrow}\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}

(E.10)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{\downarrow}\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}\right)^{+}\boldsymbol{\widehat{Q}}_{\uparrow}\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{+}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)\right)^{-1}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{-1}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\boldsymbol{I}_{r}-\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{-1}\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)\right)^{-1}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\left(-\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{-1}\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)\right)^{k}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{-1}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)\,.

where the first step follows from
Fact

A.2
with
\widehat{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{\downarrow}
being full column rank and
\boldsymbol{P}
being invertible, the second step follows from Eq. (
5.7
), the third step follows from the definition of the pseudoinverse, the fourth step is by direct calculation, the fifth step follows from pulling out the term
\boldsymbol{Q}^{\dagger}_{\downarrow}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}
, and the last step follows from Neumann series expansion.

By
Claim

E.4
, the Neumann series in Eq. (
E.10
) can be truncated up to second order:

\displaystyle\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\left(-\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{-1}\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)\right)^{k}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\underbrace{\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{-1}}_{\text{zeroth order term}}-\underbrace{\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)}_{\text{first order term}}+\underbrace{\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{2}}_{\text{second order term}}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{3}\log^{3}n}{\mu^{3}_{r}(\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n)^{1.5}}\right)\,.

Then, we can plug it into Eq. (
E.10
) to simplify
\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{\widehat{Q}}^{+}_{\downarrow}\boldsymbol{\widehat{Q}}_{\uparrow}\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{Q}^{+}_{\downarrow}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}
and drop the terms containing more than two
\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}
or
\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}
. We first consider the zeroth order term:

\displaystyle\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{-1}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)-\boldsymbol{Q}^{+}_{\downarrow}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}

(E.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{-1}\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)+\left(\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{-1}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\right)\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\left\|(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}(\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n)^{1.5}}\right)\,,

where the first step follows from
(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{-1}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}=\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{+}
by the definition of pseudoinverse, the second step is straightforward, the third step follows from Eq. (
E.18
), and the last step follows from Eq. (
5.4
). For the first order term, we have

\displaystyle\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

(E.12)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

\displaystyle+\left\|(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)+\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{3}\log^{3}n}{\mu^{3}_{r}(\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n)^{1.5}}\right)\,,

where the second step uses Eq. (
5.4
) for
\|\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\|_{2}
and Eq. (
E.18
) for
\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\|_{2}
. Similarly, for the second order term, we have

\displaystyle\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

(E.13)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{2}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\left\|\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{2}\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}_{\uparrow}^{Q}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{2}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{3}\log^{3}n}{\mu^{3}_{r}(\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n)^{1.5}}\right)\,.

And for the residual term, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{3}\log^{3}n}{\mu^{3}_{r}(\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n)^{1.5}}\right)\cdot\left\|\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{3}\log^{3}n}{\mu^{3}_{r}(\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n)^{1.5}}\right)\,.

(E.14)

Finally, combining
Eqs.

E.11
,
E.12
,
E.13
and
E.14
together, we obtain that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{\widehat{Q}}^{+}_{\downarrow}\boldsymbol{\widehat{Q}}_{\uparrow}\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{Q}^{+}_{\downarrow}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

\displaystyle+\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

\displaystyle+\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{2}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{3}\log^{3}n}{\mu^{3}_{r}(\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n)^{1.5}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)-\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}

\displaystyle+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow})-\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(-(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})+\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{2}\right)(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{3}\log^{3}n}{\mu^{3}_{r}(\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n)^{1.5}}\right)\,,

where we re-group the terms in the second step.

The proof of the lemma is completed.
∎

See
5.4

Proof.

We first deal with the first order term in Eq. (
5.8
):

\displaystyle\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)-\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}

(E.15)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}-\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)}_{\mathrm{(I)}}+\underbrace{\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}-\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)}_{\mathrm{(II)}}\,.

To bound
\mathrm{(I)}
, we notice

\textbf{R}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow})=\textbf{R}(\left(\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\right)_{\uparrow})=\textbf{R}(\left(\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\right)_{\downarrow})=\textbf{R}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})\,,

where the second step follows from
\left(\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\right)_{\uparrow}=\left(\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\right)_{\downarrow}\cdot\diag(\boldsymbol{z}_{\rm dom})
.
We also notice that

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}-\Pi_{\textbf{R}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow})}\right\|_{2}=

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}-\Pi_{\textbf{R}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})}\right\|_{2}

(E.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}-\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\boldsymbol{P}_{Q_{\downarrow}}\boldsymbol{P}^{\dagger}_{Q_{\downarrow}}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\right\|_{2}\cdot\left\|\boldsymbol{I}_{r}-\boldsymbol{P}_{Q_{\downarrow}}\boldsymbol{P}^{\dagger}_{Q_{\downarrow}}\right\|_{2}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\,,

where
\Pi_{\textbf{R}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})}
denotes the projector to the column space of
\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}
, the second step follows the definition of
\boldsymbol{P}_{Q_{\downarrow}}
Eq. (
E.20
), and the last step follows from Eq. (
E.19
) and
\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\|_{2}={\cal O}(1)
.

Combining this and Eq. (
5.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}-\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)\Big\|_{2}=

\displaystyle\Big\|(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}-\Pi_{\textbf{R}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow})}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)+(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\left(\Pi_{\textbf{R}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow})}-\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\right)\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\Big\|_{2}

(E.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\|(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\left(\Pi_{\textbf{R}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow})}-\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\right)\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\big\|\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\big\|_{2}\cdot\left\|\Pi_{\textbf{R}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow})}-\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}\cdot\left\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}}\right)\cdot\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}(\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n)^{1.5}}\right)\,,

where the first step is straightforward, the second step follows from
\Pi_{\textbf{R}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow})}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}=\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}
by definition, the third step follows from triangle inequality, the fourth step follows from Eq. (
5.4
) and Eq. (
E.16
).

Next, we keep
\mathrm{(II)}
and first consider the second-order term in Eq. (
5.8
), which can be rewritten as follows:

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow})-\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(-(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})+\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{2}\right)(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}-\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

\displaystyle-\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)\left(\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)-\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}-\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)

\displaystyle-\left((\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)\Bigg(\underbrace{(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}-\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)}_{\mathrm{(I)}~\text{in~Eq.~\eqref{eqn:first_order_derivation}}}+\underbrace{\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}-\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)}_{\mathrm{(II)}~\text{in~Eq.~\eqref{eqn:first_order_derivation}}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}-\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}}\right)\left\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}-\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}\log n}{\mu^{2}_{r}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}n^{2}}\right)

where the first two steps follow from re-grouping the terms, and the last step follows from Eq. (
E.22
) and Eq. (
E.17
).

Plugging the bounds for the first order and the second order terms into Eq. (
5.8
), we get that

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{P}\boldsymbol{\widehat{Q}}^{+}_{\downarrow}\boldsymbol{\widehat{Q}}_{\uparrow}\boldsymbol{P}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{Q}^{+}_{\downarrow}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\|(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}-\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)\right\|_{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(1+\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\log n}}{\mu_{r}\sqrt{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}}\right)\left\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\uparrow}-\boldsymbol{E}^{Q}_{\downarrow}(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\uparrow}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\alpha^{3}\log^{3}n}{\mu^{3}_{r}(\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n)^{1.5}}\right)\,,

which proves the lemma.
∎

E.2.1
Technical claims

Claim E.3
(Approximate unitarity of
\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}
).

For
\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}=\boldsymbol{Q}(1:,:r)
, we have

\left\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right),\quad\left\|\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{-1}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\,.

(E.18)

Furthermore, there exists a matrix
\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}}\in\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}
such that
\boldsymbol{P}^{\dagger}_{Q_{\downarrow}}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\boldsymbol{P}_{Q_{\downarrow}}=\boldsymbol{I}_{r}
and

\left\|\boldsymbol{P}^{\dagger}_{Q_{\downarrow}}\boldsymbol{P}_{Q_{\downarrow}}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right),\quad\left\|\boldsymbol{P}_{Q_{\downarrow}}\boldsymbol{P}^{\dagger}_{Q_{\downarrow}}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\,.

(E.19)

Proof.

We first simplify
\left(\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}\right)^{-1}
in the above formula:

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\downarrow}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\right\|_{2}=

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{P}^{\dagger}_{v}\frac{\left(\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\right)_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\right)_{\downarrow}}{n}\boldsymbol{P}_{v}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{P}^{\dagger}_{v}\boldsymbol{P}_{v}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\boldsymbol{P}_{v}^{\dagger}\left(\frac{\left(\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\right)_{\downarrow}^{\dagger}\left(\boldsymbol{V}_{n}(\boldsymbol{z}_{\mathrm{dom}})\right)_{\downarrow}}{n}-\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\right)\boldsymbol{P}_{v}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}n}\right)\,.

where the first step follows from Eq. (
1.13
) that
